#ifndef OPENSSL_HEADER_EC_ECP_NISTZ384_H
#define OPENSSL_HEADER_EC_ECP_NISTZ384_H

#include "../../limbs/limbs.h"

#define P384_LIMBS (384u / LIMB_BITS)

  Limb X[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Y[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Z[P384_LIMBS];
} P384_POINT;

//! EdDSA Signatures.

use super::ops::ELEM_LEN;
use crate::digest;

pub mod signing;
pub mod verification;

/// The length of an Ed25519 public key.
pub const ED25519_PUBLIC_KEY_LEN: usize = ELEM_LEN;

pub fn eddsa_digest(signature_r: &[u8], public_key: &[u8], msg: &[u8]) -> digest::Digest {
    let mut ctx = digest::Context::new(&digest::SHA512);
    ctx.update(signature_r);
    ctx.update(public_key);
    ctx.update(msg);
    ctx.finish()
}

use crate::limb::Limb;

#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
#[repr(transparent)]
pub struct N0([Limb; 2]);

impl N0 {
    #[cfg(feature = "alloc")]
    pub(super) const LIMBS_USED: usize = 64 / crate::limb::LIMB_BITS;

    #[inline]
    pub const fn precalculated(n0: u64) -> Self {
        #[cfg(target_pointer_width = "64")]
        {
            Self([n0, 0])
        }

        #[cfg(target_pointer_width = "32")]
        {
            Self([n0 as Limb, (n0 >> crate::limb::LIMB_BITS) as Limb])
        }
    }
}

use crate::error::{KeyRejected, Unspecified};

impl From<untrusted::EndOfInput> for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: untrusted::EndOfInput) -> Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

impl From<core::array::TryFromSliceError> for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: core::array::TryFromSliceError) -> Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

impl From<KeyRejected> for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: KeyRejected) -> Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

// MERCHANTABILITY AND FITNESS. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE AUTHOR BE LIABLE FOR ANY
// SPECIAL, DIRECT, INDIRECT, OR CONSEQUENTIAL DAMAGES OR ANY DAMAGES
// WHATSOEVER RESULTING FROM LOSS OF USE, DATA OR PROFITS, WHETHER IN AN ACTION
// OF CONTRACT, NEGLIGENCE OR OTHER TORTIOUS ACTION, ARISING OUT OF OR IN
// CONNECTION WITH THE USE OR PERFORMANCE OF THIS SOFTWARE.

#[cfg(target_arch = "x86")]
pub fn shift_full_blocks<const BLOCK_LEN: usize>(
    in_out: super::overlapping::Overlapping<'_, u8>,
    mut transform: impl FnMut(&[u8; BLOCK_LEN]) -> [u8; BLOCK_LEN],
) {
    let (in_out, src) = in_out.into_slice_src_mut();
    let in_out_len = in_out[src.clone()].len();

    for i in (0..in_out_len).step_by(BLOCK_LEN) {
        let block = {
            let input =
                <&[u8; BLOCK_LEN]>::try_from(&in_out[(src.start + i)..][..BLOCK_LEN]).unwrap();
            transform(input)
        };
        let output = <&mut [u8; BLOCK_LEN]>::try_from(&mut in_out[i..][..BLOCK_LEN]).unwrap();
        *output = block;
    }
}

/// Polyfill for `Option::unwrap()` as a const fn; feature `const_option`.
/// https://github.com/rust-lang/rust/issues/67441.
/// TODO(MSRV): Replace this with `x.unwrap()`.
///
/// `T: Copy` avoids "constant functions cannot evaluate destructors."
pub const fn unwrap_const<T>(x: Option<T>) -> T
where
    T: Copy,
{
    if let Some(x) = x {
        x
    } else {
        panic!("unwrap_const on `None`");
    }
}

//! Utilities to make dealing with slices less tediuous.

/// Replaces the first N elements of `a` with the first N elements of `b`, where
/// N is `core::cmp::min(a.len(), b.len())`, leaving the rest unchanged.
pub fn overwrite_at_start<T: Copy>(a: &mut [T], b: &[T]) {
    a.iter_mut().zip(b).for_each(|(a, b)| {
        *a = *b;
    });
}

pub fn escape(text: &str) -> String {
    let mut quoted = String::new();
    escape_into(text, &mut quoted);
    quoted
}

fn escape_into(text: &str, buf: &mut String) {
    buf.reserve(text.len());
    for c in text.chars() {
        if is_meta_character(c) {
            buf.push('\\');
        }
        buf.push(c);
    }
}

fn is_meta_character(c: char) -> bool {
    match c {
        '\\' | '.' | '+' | '*' | '?' | '(' | ')' | '|' | '[' | ']' | '{' | '}' | '^' | '$'
        | '#' | '&' | '-' | '~' => true,
        _ => false,
    }
}

int LLVMFuzzerTestOneInput(const uint8_t *data, size_t size) {
	int w, h;
	if (size < 4) {
		return 0;
	}

	qoi_desc desc;
	void* decoded = qoi_decode((void*)(data + 4), (int)(size - 4), &desc, *((int *)data));
	if (decoded != NULL) {
		free(decoded);
	}
	return 0;
}

Native libraries in the speech engine

The nixa-voice crate links these C and C++ libraries from the prebuilt sherpa-onnx v2.0.2 static archive (osx-arm64). They are not Rust crates, so they are listed here by hand.

Kaldi COPYING

Used by:

  • kaldi-hmm-gmm, kaldifst and kaldi-native-io carry code from Kaldi (github.com/kaldi-asr/kaldi)
Update to legal notice, made Feb 2012, modified Sep 2013.  We would like to
 clarify that we are using a convention where multiple names in the Apache
 copyright headers, for example

  // Copyright 2009-2012  Yanmin Qian  Arnab Ghoshal
  //                2013  Vassil Panayotov

 does not signify joint ownership of copyright of that file, except in cases
 where all those names were present in the original release made in March 2011--
 you can use the version history to work this out, if this matters to you.
 Instead, we intend that those contributors who later modified the file, agree
 to release their changes under the Apache license.  The conventional way of
 signifying this is to duplicate the Apache headers at the top of each file each
 time a change is made by a different author, but this would quickly become
 impractical.

 Where the copyright header says something like:

 // Copyright    2013   Johns Hopkins University (author: Daniel Povey)

 it is because the individual who wrote the code was at that institution as an
 employee, so the copyright is owned by the university (and we will have checked
 that the contributions were in accordance with the open-source policies of the
 institutions concerned, including getting them vetted individually where
 necessary).  From a legal point of view the copyright ownership is that of the
 institution concerned, and the (author: xxx) in parentheses is just
 informational, to identify the actual person who wrote the code, and is not
 intended to have any legal implications.  In some cases, however, particularly
 early on, we just wrote the name of the university or company concerned,
 without the actual author's name in parentheses.  If you see something like

 //  Copyright  2009-2012   Arnab Ghoshal  Microsoft Corporation

 it does not imply that Arnab was working for Microsoft, it is because someone
 else contributed to the file while working at Microsoft (this would be Daniel
 Povey, in fact, who was working at Microsoft Research at the outset of the
 project).

 The list of authors of each file is in an essentially arbitrary order, but is
 often chronological if they contributed in different years.

 The original legal notice is below.  Note: we are continuing to modify it by
 adding the names of new contributors, but at any given time, the list may
 be out of date.

and distribution as defined by Sections 1 through 9 of this document.

outstanding shares, or (iii) beneficial ownership of such entity.

exercising permissions granted by this License.

"Source" form shall mean the preferred form for making modifications,
including but not limited to software source code, documentation
source, and configuration files.

SQLite Is Public Domain

All of the code and documentation in SQLite has been dedicated to the public
domain by the authors. All code authors, and representatives of the companies
they work for, have signed affidavits dedicating their contributions to the
public domain and originals of those signed affidavits are stored in a firesafe
at the main offices of Hwaci. Anyone is free to copy, modify, publish, use,
compile, sell, or distribute the original SQLite code, either in source code
form or as a compiled binary, for any purpose, commercial or non-commercial,
and by any means.

The previous paragraph applies to the deliverable code and documentation in
SQLite - those parts of the SQLite library that you actually bundle and ship
with a larger application. Some scripts used as part of the build process (for
example the "configure" scripts generated by autoconf) might fall under other
open-source licenses. Nothing from these build scripts ever reaches the final
deliverable SQLite library, however, and so the licenses associated with those
scripts should not be a factor in assessing your rights to copy and use the
SQLite library.

All of the deliverable code in SQLite has been written from scratch. No code
has been taken from other projects or from the open internet. Every line of
code can be traced back to its original author, and all of those authors have
public domain dedications on file. So the SQLite code base is clean and is
uncontaminated with licensed code from other projects.

Icons, themes, speech models and engine libraries

## Icons and pictures

- crates/ui/assets/icons: Lucide icons (lucide.dev), ISC license. Some files are saved under short Nixa names (for example chat.svg, files.svg, tasks.svg). A few were drawn new in the same style. Some Lucide icons come from the Feather project and are also under the MIT license of Cole Bemis. The full Lucide license text is at the end of this page. It must ship with the app.
- crates/app/src/screens/nixa_screen/mascot.svg: the Nixa mascot. Own artwork of the project, MIT like the rest of the code.
- crates/xtask/assets/AppIcon.icns and crates/xtask/assets/icon.svg: the app icon, made from the Nixa mascot by `cargo run -p xtask -- icon`. Own artwork, MIT like the rest of the code.

## Themes

- crates/ui/themes/*.json: colour files of Nixa, MIT.
- atom-one-dark.json uses the colours of the Atom One Dark theme. Atom One Dark: MIT, Copyright (c) 2016 GitHub Inc.
- one-dark-pro.json uses the colours of the One Dark Pro theme (github.com/Binaryify/OneDark-Pro). One Dark Pro: MIT.
- atom-green, graphite, ink, matrix and moss are own colour sets.

## Fonts

- No font files ship with the app.
- The window uses Menlo, a macOS system font.
- The terminal uses JetBrainsMono Nerd Font or JetBrains Mono when the user has installed one, else Menlo.

## Speech models

The models are not in the app or in this repo. Nixa downloads them only after the user says yes, from the sherpa-onnx model releases on GitHub (github.com/k2-fsa/sherpa-onnx/releases/tag/asr-models), and checks each file with SHA-256.

- silero_vad.onnx (voice activity): Silero VAD, MIT license, Copyright (c) 2020-present Silero Team (github.com/snakers4/silero-vad).
- sherpa-onnx-nemo-parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-int8 (speech to text): Parakeet TDT 0.6B v3 by NVIDIA (huggingface.co/nvidia/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3), CC BY 4.0 license. Converted to ONNX int8 by the sherpa-onnx project. Nixa does not change the model.
- Attribution line for the speech to text model, to show where the model is named (first run voice step, Settings, Mic and speaker): "Speech to text: Parakeet TDT 0.6B v3 by NVIDIA, CC BY 4.0 (creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0). Voice activity: Silero VAD by the Silero Team, MIT."

## Speech engine libraries

- sherpa-onnx 2.0.2 and sherpa-onnx-sys 2.0.2 (nixa-voice): Apache-2.0.
- The first build of nixa-voice downloads the prebuilt sherpa-onnx v2.0.2 static archive (osx-arm64) and links these libraries into the app:
  - sherpa-onnx core and C API: Apache-2.0 (k2-fsa).
  - kaldi-decoder, kaldi-native-fbank: Apache-2.0.
  - kaldifst, kaldi-native-io, kaldi-hmm-gmm: Apache-2.0 with the Kaldi legal notices.
  - OpenFst (sherpa-onnx-fst, sherpa-onnx-fstfar): Apache-2.0.
  - simple-sentencepiece (ssentencepiece_core): Apache-2.0.
  - kissfft: BSD-3-Clause, Copyright (c) 2003-2010 Mark Borgerding.
  - utf8proc: MIT and the Unicode data license.
  - onnxruntime 1.28: MIT, Copyright (c) Microsoft Corporation, with its ThirdPartyNotices.
- The archive has no license files. Their full texts are in the "Native libraries in the speech engine" part at the end of docs/licenses-full.html. The texts are written into about.hbs, taken from each project's own repo, and the onnxruntime LICENSE and ThirdPartyNotices.txt from its 1.28.0 package on PyPI.

## Text to speech

- macOS system voices by default. They are part of macOS and do not ship with Nixa.
- ptts only when the user has installed it. Not bundled.

## Outside tools that Nixa starts but does not ship

- Coding agents (Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Cursor and others): the user installs them under their own terms. Nixa shows agent names as text only, no agent logos.
- Playwright MCP (@playwright/mcp, Apache-2.0, Microsoft): browser actions, started by npx only when the user turns browser actions on.
- SearXNG (AGPL-3.0): local web search, a separate process the user installs. Nixa only sends it HTTP requests. It does not link or ship its code.
- git and gh: the user's own installs.
- macOS frameworks (Vision, Accessibility, CoreGraphics, AVFoundation, CoreAudio) are used through objc2. They are part of macOS.

## Notes on licenses

- No GPL, LGPL or AGPL crate is in the app.
- MPL-2.0: option-ext 0.2.0 is in the app, used unchanged (file-level copyleft only; its source is on crates.io). cbindgen 0.28.0 is build only.
- libgit2 (through git2 0.20.4 and libgit2-sys 0.18.8) is tests only. Its C code is GPL-2.0 with a linking exception. It is not in the app.
- CDLA-Permissive-2.0: webpki-roots 0.26.11 and 1.0.12, build only.
- bzip2-1.0.6: libbz2-rs-sys 0.2.7, build only.
- Unicode-3.0: the icu_*, zerovec, zerofrom, zerotrie, yoke, litemap, tinystr, potential_utf and writeable crates in the app, and unicode-properties. Their license text must ship with the app.
- aws-lc-rs and aws-lc-sys are in the app (the default TLS provider of rustls, used by reqwest). aws-lc-sys is ISC AND (Apache-2.0 OR ISC) AND Apache-2.0 AND MIT AND BSD-3-Clause AND (Apache-2.0 OR ISC OR MIT) AND (Apache-2.0 OR ISC OR MIT-0). Its license text must ship with the app.
- ring 0.17.14 is Apache-2.0 AND ISC. Its license text must ship with the app.
- Crates under two or more licenses (for example MIT OR Apache-2.0) are used under MIT where MIT is one of the choices.
- CC0-1.0, MIT-0 and Unlicense choices have no conditions.
- gpui is built with default features off and features font-kit and runtime_shaders, so no Linux or Windows parts are pulled in.
- The gpui test-support feature is a dev dependency only. Its crates are in the tests only list.
